I'll take some pan dulce out to the garage and eat that, too. I end up eating a whole lot of food—way more than I’d ever actually put on a plate if my family was sitting there watching me.\n\nDoctor: Does this loss of control over eating happen at other times of the day, or is it mostly at night?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just at night. During the day, I eat my normal meals with my wife, maybe a small snack, and I feel fine. I don't feel that urge to just keep going. It's only after supper, when the house is quiet and I'm cleaning up the kitchen or heading out to the garage, that it happens.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I don't really feel stressed or anxious about much these days. I'm not working anymore, so my days are pretty quiet and simple. During the day, I eat my normal meals with my wife, maybe a small snack, and I feel fine. I don't feel that urge to just keep going. It's only after supper, when the house is quiet and I'm cleaning up the kitchen or heading out to the garage, that it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating quickly when you're having one of these episodes at night?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I eat quickly. It's more of a... a steady thing. I just keep picking at it, taking another bite, then another. I mean, I've already had a good dinner with everyone, so I'm usually pretty full. But later on... I don't usually get sick or anything like that, but... I'll have eaten enough that my belt feels pretty tight. Some nights after supper, when I’m supposed to be putting away the leftovers—like the tamales or the mole—I’ll end up eating several extra servings. And then I'll usually take some pan dulce out to the garage with me, too. I wouldn't say I eat fast. I just kind of pick at things... take a bite of this, a piece of that. I'm taking my time with it, really. I've been having this problem with eating lately. \n\nOnce I start picking at the leftovers late at night, it's like... it feels like the sensible part of me is sitting right there saying, 'That’s enough, Alejandro,' but my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. I just can't seem to stop myself once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating late at night like that?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I feel terrible about it. I mean, I know I shouldn't be eating like that, especially at my age. It just feels foolish, and I definitely feel guilty once I finally stop and see what I've done.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat late at night because you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I don't really feel stressed or anxious about much these days. I'm not working anymore, so my days are pretty quiet and simple. It doesn't really seem tied to feeling worried or anything like that... I just find myself doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating when you're picking at leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Like I mentioned before, it's like I know I should stop... I tell myself to stop... but I just keep eating anyway. I don't feel like I have any control over it once I get started on those leftovers.\n\nDoctor: Is it always leftovers that you lose control over eating, or can it happen with other types of food as well?\n\nPatient: Well, it usually starts with the leftovers, but then it turns into other things. Like, some nights after supper, when I’m just supposed to be putting away the tamales or the mole, I’ll end up eating several extra servings of that. And then... I'll take some pan dulce out to the garage and eat that, too. I end up eating a whole lot of food—way more than I’d ever actually put on a plate if my family was sitting there watching me.\n\nDoctor: Does this loss of control over eating happen at other times of the day, or is it mostly at night?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just at night. During the day, I eat my normal meals with my wife, maybe a small snack, and I feel fine. I don't feel that urge to just keep going. It's only after supper, when the house is quiet and I'm cleaning up the kitchen or heading out to the garage, that it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating quickly when you're having one of these episodes at night?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I eat quickly. It's more of a... a steady thing. I just keep picking at it, taking another bite, then another. It's not like I'm rushing to get it down, I just can't seem to stop the process once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel very hungry before you start eating those leftovers at night?\n\nPatient: No, a lot of the time I'm not even hungry. I mean, I've already had a good dinner with everyone, so I'm usually pretty full. But later on... I just still find myself opening containers and tasting one thing after another, even without feeling hungry at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself feeling uncomfortably full after one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I don't usually get sick or anything like that, but... I'll have eaten enough that my belt feels pretty tight. I usually just end up having to sit in my workshop chair for a while because my stomach gets so uncomfortable.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat a large amount of food during these episodes, more than you would normally eat in one sitting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say it's a large amount. Some nights after supper, when I’m supposed to be putting away the leftovers—like the tamales or the mole—I’ll end up eating several extra servings. And then I'll usually take some pan dulce out to the garage with me, too. When you add it all up, it's definitely way more food than I’d ever put on a plate if the family was around watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating rapidly during these episodes at night?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I eat fast. I just kind of pick at things... take a bite of this, a piece of that. I'm taking my time with it, really. It's just that I keep eating for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually try to hide or keep secret the fact that you're eating these extra amounts of food at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I make sure I wait until the house is quiet, or I take it out to the garage with me. I'd just be... well, I'd be embarrassed if my cousin or the rest of the family saw how much I was actually eating. I'm supposed to be the disciplined one, you know? So I definitely keep it to myself.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."

you're eating rapidly during these episodes at night?\n\nPatient: No, not really. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello, doctor. Well, what brings me in is... I've been having this problem with eating lately. \n\nOnce I start picking at the leftovers late at night, it's like... it feels like the sensible part of me is sitting right there saying, 'That’s enough, Alejandro,' but my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. I just can't seem to stop myself once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating late at night like that?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I feel terrible about it. I mean, I know I shouldn't be eating like that, especially at my age. It just feels foolish, and I definitely feel guilty once I finally stop and see what I've done.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat late at night because you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I don't really feel stressed or anxious about much these days. I'm not working anymore, so my days are pretty quiet and simple. It doesn't really seem tied to feeling worried or anything like that... I just find myself doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating when you're picking at leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Like I mentioned before, it's like I know I should stop... I tell myself to stop... but I just keep eating anyway. I don't feel like I have any control over it once I get started on those leftovers.\n\nDoctor: Is it always leftovers that you lose control over eating, or can it happen with other types of food as well?\n\nPatient: Well, it usually starts with the leftovers, but then it turns into other things. Like, some nights after supper, when I’m just supposed to be putting away the tamales or the mole, I’ll end up eating several extra servings of that. And then... I'll take some pan dulce out to the garage and eat that, too. I end up eating a whole lot of food—way more than I’d ever actually put on a plate if my family was sitting there watching me.\n\nDoctor: Does this loss of control over eating happen at other times of the day, or is it mostly at night?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just at night. During the day, I eat my normal meals with my wife, maybe a small snack, and I feel fine. I don't feel that urge to just keep going. It's only after supper, when the house is quiet and I'm cleaning up the kitchen or heading out to the garage, that it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating quickly when you're having one of these episodes at night?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I eat quickly. It's more of a... a steady thing. I just keep picking at it, taking another bite, then another. It's not like I'm rushing to get it down, I just can't seem to stop the process once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel very hungry before you start eating those leftovers at night?\n\nPatient: No, a lot of the time I'm not even hungry. I mean, I've already had a good dinner with everyone, so I'm usually pretty full. But later on... I just still find myself opening containers and tasting one thing after another, even without feeling hungry at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself feeling uncomfortably full after one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I don't usually get sick or anything like that, but... I'll have eaten enough that my belt feels pretty tight. I usually just end up having to sit in my workshop chair for a while because my stomach gets so uncomfortable.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat a large amount of food during these episodes, more than you would normally eat in one sitting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say it's a large amount. Some nights after supper, when I’m supposed to be putting away the leftovers—like the tamales or the mole—I’ll end up eating several extra servings. And then I'll usually take some pan dulce out to the garage with me, too. When you add it all up, it's definitely way more food than I’d ever put on a plate if the family was around watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating rapidly during these episodes at night?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I eat fast. I just kind of pick at things... take a bite of this, a piece of that. I'm taking my time with it, really. It's just that I keep eating for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually try to hide or keep secret the fact that you're eating these extra amounts of food at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I make sure I wait until the house is quiet, or I take it out to the garage with me. I'd just be... well, I'd be embarrassed if my cousin or the rest of the family saw how much I was actually eating. I'm supposed to be the disciplined one, you know? So I definitely keep it to myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating at night because you're trying to feel better or comfort yourself in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that. I haven't really thought about it like that, to be honest. I just know I end up eating all that extra food when I'm out there working on my projects or when the house is quiet. I don't know if I'm trying to comfort myself... it's just something I end up doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating at night helps you feel better emotionally?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying, I really don't know. I don't think it makes me feel better, no. I haven't noticed any kind of emotional lift from it or anything like that. It's just... well, it's just a habit I've fallen into when I'm out there. I don't really connect it to my mood.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.     "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.4, F50.5\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello, doctor. Well, what brings me in is... I've been having this problem with eating lately. \n\nOnce I start picking at the leftovers late at night, it's like... it feels like the sensible part of me is sitting right there saying, 'That’s enough, Alejandro,' but my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. I just can't seem to stop myself once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating late at night like that?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I feel terrible about it. I mean, I know I shouldn't be eating like that, especially at my age. It just feels foolish, and I definitely feel guilty once I finally stop and see what I've done.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat late at night because you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I don't really feel stressed or anxious about much these days. I'm not working anymore, so my days are pretty quiet and simple. It doesn't really seem tied to feeling worried or anything like that... I just find myself doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating when you're picking at leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Like I mentioned before, it's like I know I should stop... I tell myself to stop... but I just keep eating anyway. I don't feel like I have any control over it once I get started on those leftovers.\n\nDoctor: Is it always leftovers that you lose control over eating, or can it happen with other types of food as well?\n\nPatient: Well, it usually starts with the leftovers, but then it turns into other things. Like, some nights after supper, when I’m just supposed to be putting away the tamales or the mole, I’ll end up eating several extra servings of that. And then... I'll take some pan dulce out to the garage and eat that, too. I end up eating a whole lot of food—way more than I’d ever actually put on a plate if my family was sitting there watching me.\n\nDoctor: Does this loss of control over eating happen at other times of the day, or is it mostly at night?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just at night. During the day, I eat my normal meals with my wife, maybe a small snack, and I feel fine. I don't feel that urge to just keep going. It's only after supper, when the house is quiet and I'm cleaning up the kitchen or heading out to the garage, that it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating quickly when you're having one of these episodes at night?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I eat quickly. It's more of a... a steady thing. I just keep picking at it, taking another bite, then another. It's not like I'm rushing to get it down, I just can't seem to stop the process once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel very hungry before you start eating those leftovers at night?\n\nPatient: No, a lot of the time I'm not even hungry. I mean, I've already had a good dinner with everyone, so I'm usually pretty full. But later on... I just still find myself opening containers and tasting one thing after another, even without feeling hungry at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself feeling uncomfortably full after one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I don't usually get sick or anything like that, but... I'll have eaten enough that my belt feels pretty tight. I usually just end up having to sit in my workshop chair for a while because my stomach gets so uncomfortable.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat a large amount of food during these episodes, more than you would normally eat in one sitting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say it's a large amount. Some nights after supper, when I’m supposed to be putting away the leftovers—like the tamales or the mole—I’ll end up eating several extra servings. And then I'll usually take some pan dulce out to the garage with me, too. When you add it all up, it's definitely way more food than I’d ever put on a plate if the family was around watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating rapidly during these episodes at night?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I eat fast. I just kind of pick at things... take a bite of this, a piece of that. I'm taking my time with it, really. It's just that I keep eating for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually try to hide or keep secret the fact that you're eating these extra amounts of food at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I make sure I wait until the house is quiet, or I take it out to the garage with me. I'd just be... well, I'd be embarrassed if my cousin or the rest of the family saw how much I was actually eating. I'm supposed to be the disciplined one, you know? So I definitely keep it to myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating at night because you're trying to feel better or comfort yourself in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that. I haven't really thought about it like that, to be honest. I just know I end up eating all that extra food when I'm out there working on my projects or when the house is quiet. I don't know if I'm trying to comfort myself... it's just something I end up doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating at night helps you feel better emotionally?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying, I really don't know. I don't think it makes me feel better, no. I haven't noticed any kind of emotional lift from it or anything like that. It's just... well, it's just a habit I've fallen into when I'm out there. I don't really connect it to my mood.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still eat this way at night if you were in a very good mood?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I probably would. Like I said, I don't really think my mood has much to do with it. Even if I've had a great day, once it gets late and I'm out in the garage... I still end up eating all that extra food. It just seems to happen regardless of how I'm feeling.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I end up eating a whole lot of food—way more than I’d ever actually put on a plate if my family was sitting there watching me.\n\nDoctor: Does this loss of control over eating happen at other times of the day, or is it mostly at night?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just at night. During the day, I eat my normal meals with my wife, maybe a small snack, and I feel fine. I don't feel that urge to just keep going. It's only after supper, when the house is quiet and I'm cleaning up the kitchen or heading out to the garage, that it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating quickly when you're having one of these episodes at night?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I eat quickly. It's more of a... a steady thing. I just keep picking at it, taking another bite, then another. It's not like I'm rushing to get it down, I just can't seem to stop the process once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel very hungry before you start eating those leftovers at night?\n\nPatient: No, a lot of the time I'm not even hungry. I mean, I've already had a good dinner with everyone, so I'm usually pretty full. But later on... I just still find myself opening containers and tasting one thing after another, even without feeling hungry at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself feeling uncomfortably full after one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I don't usually get sick or anything like that, but... I'll have eaten enough that my belt feels pretty tight. I usually just end up having to sit in my workshop chair for a while because my stomach gets so uncomfortable.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat a large amount of food during these episodes, more than you would normally eat in one sitting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say it's a large amount. Some nights after supper, when I’m supposed to be putting away the leftovers—like the tamales or the mole—I’ll end up eating several extra servings. And then I'll usually take some pan dulce out to the garage with me, too. When you add it all up, it's definitely way more food than I’d ever put on a plate if the family was around watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating rapidly during these episodes at night?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I eat fast. I just kind of pick at things... take a bite of this, a piece of that. I'm taking my time with it, really. It's just that I keep eating for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually try to hide or keep secret the fact that you're eating these extra amounts of food at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I make sure I wait until the house is quiet, or I take it out to the garage with me. I'd just be... well, I'd be embarrassed if my cousin or the rest of the family saw how much I was actually eating. I'm supposed to be the disciplined one, you know? So I definitely keep it to myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating at night because you're trying to feel better or comfort yourself in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that. I haven't really thought about it like that, to be honest. I just know I end up eating all that extra food when I'm out there working on my projects or when the house is quiet. I don't know if I'm trying to comfort myself... it's just something I end up doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating at night helps you feel better emotionally?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying, I really don't know. I don't think it makes me feel better, no. I haven't noticed any kind of emotional lift from it or anything like that. It's just... well, it's just a habit I've fallen into when I'm out there. I don't really connect it to my mood.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still eat this way at night if you were in a very good mood?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I probably would. Like I said, I don't really think my mood has much to do with it. Even if I've had a great day, once it gets late and I'm out in the garage... I still end up eating all that extra food. It just seems to happen regardless of how I'm feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat less at night if you weren't alone?\n\nPatient: Oh, definitely. I mean, I actually make sure I do it after the house is quiet or when I'm out in the garage. I'd be really embarrassed for my cousin or the rest of my family to see just how much I'm eating. I'm supposed to be the disciplined one, you know? So, yeah, if someone else was around, I wouldn't be doing it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's more of a... a steady thing. I just keep picking at it, taking another bite, then another. I don't think it makes me feel better, no. I haven't noticed any kind of emotional lift from it or anything like that. It's just... well, it's just a habit I've fallen into when I'm out there. I don't really connect it to my mood.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still eat this way at night if you were in a very good mood?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I probably would. Like I said, I don't really think my mood has much to do with it. Even if I've had a great day, once it gets late and I'm out in the garage... I still end up eating all that extra food. I end up eating a whole lot of food—way more than I’d ever actually put on a plate if my family was sitting there watching me.\n\nDoctor: Does this loss of control over eating happen at other times of the day, or is it mostly at night?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just at night. During the day, I eat my normal meals with my wife, maybe a small snack, and I feel fine. I don't feel that urge to just keep going. It's only after supper, when the house is quiet and I'm cleaning up the kitchen or heading out to the garage, that it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating quickly when you're having one of these episodes at night?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I eat quickly. It's more of a... a steady thing. I just keep picking at it, taking another bite, then another. It's not like I'm rushing to get it down, I just can't seem to stop the process once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel very hungry before you start eating those leftovers at night?\n\nPatient: No, a lot of the time I'm not even hungry. I mean, I've already had a good dinner with everyone, so I'm usually pretty full. But later on... I just still find myself opening containers and tasting one thing after another, even without feeling hungry at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself feeling uncomfortably full after one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I don't usually get sick or anything like that, but... I'll have eaten enough that my belt feels pretty tight. I usually just end up having to sit in my workshop chair for a while because my stomach gets so uncomfortable.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat a large amount of food during these episodes, more than you would normally eat in one sitting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say it's a large amount. Some nights after supper, when I’m supposed to be putting away the leftovers—like the tamales or the mole—I’ll end up eating several extra servings. And then I'll usually take some pan dulce out to the garage with me, too. When you add it all up, it's definitely way more food than I’d ever put on a plate if the family was around watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating rapidly during these episodes at night?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I eat fast. I just kind of pick at things... take a bite of this, a piece of that. I'm taking my time with it, really. It's just that I keep eating for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually try to hide or keep secret the fact that you're eating these extra amounts of food at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I make sure I wait until the house is quiet, or I take it out to the garage with me. I'd just be... well, I'd be embarrassed if my cousin or the rest of the family saw how much I was actually eating. I'm supposed to be the disciplined one, you know? So I definitely keep it to myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating at night because you're trying to feel better or comfort yourself in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that. I haven't really thought about it like that, to be honest. I just know I end up eating all that extra food when I'm out there working on my projects or when the house is quiet. I don't know if I'm trying to comfort myself... it's just something I end up doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating at night helps you feel better emotionally?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying, I really don't know. I don't think it makes me feel better, no. I haven't noticed any kind of emotional lift from it or anything like that. It's just... well, it's just a habit I've fallen into when I'm out there. I don't really connect it to my mood.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still eat this way at night if you were in a very good mood?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I probably would. Like I said, I don't really think my mood has much to do with it. Even if I've had a great day, once it gets late and I'm out in the garage... I still end up eating all that extra food. It just seems to happen regardless of how I'm feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat less at night if you weren't alone?\n\nPatient: Oh, definitely. I mean, I actually make sure I do it after the house is quiet or when I'm out in the garage. I'd be really embarrassed for my cousin or the rest of my family to see just how much I'm eating. I'm supposed to be the disciplined one, you know? So, yeah, if someone else was around, I wouldn't be doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're losing control over your eating during the day, not just at night?\n\nPatient: No, not really. During the day, I'm... well, I'm fine. I just eat my regular meals. It's really only at night, like I was saying, when I'm out there by myself. During the day, I don't have that feeling of losing control at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat less at night if you weren't eating to distract yourself from any thoughts or feelings?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't think that really applies to me. I'm not... I don't feel like I'm eating to block out any thoughts or feelings. Like I mentioned a minute ago, my mood doesn't really seem to factor into it at all. It's just something that happens when I'm out in the garage late at night. So, no, I don't think that would make a difference either way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."

It's just that I keep eating for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually try to hide or keep secret the fact that you're eating these extra amounts of food at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I make sure I wait until the house is quiet, or I take it out to the garage with me. I'd just be... well, I'd be embarrassed if my cousin or the rest of the family saw how much I was actually eating. I'm supposed to be the disciplined one, you know? So I definitely keep it to myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating at night because you're trying to feel better or comfort yourself in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that. I haven't really thought about it like that, to be honest. I just know I end up eating all that extra food when I'm out there working on my projects or when the house is quiet. I don't know if I'm trying to comfort myself... it's just something I end up doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating at night helps you feel better emotionally?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying, I really don't know. I don't think it makes me feel better, no. I haven't noticed any kind of emotional lift from it or anything like that. It's just... well, it's just a habit I've fallen into when I'm out there. I don't really connect it to my mood.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still eat this way at night if you were in a very good mood?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I probably would. Like I said, I don't really think my mood has much to do with it. Even if I've had a great day, once it gets late and I'm out in the garage... I still end up eating all that extra food. It just seems to happen regardless of how I'm feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat less at night if you weren't alone?\n\nPatient: Oh, definitely. I mean, I actually make sure I do it after the house is quiet or when I'm out in the garage. I'd be really embarrassed for my cousin or the rest of my family to see just how much I'm eating. I'm supposed to be the disciplined one, you know? So, yeah, if someone else was around, I wouldn't be doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're losing control over your eating during the day, not just at night?\n\nPatient: No, not really. During the day, I'm... well, I'm fine. I just eat my regular meals. It's really only at night, like I was saying, when I'm out there by myself. During the day, I don't have that feeling of losing control at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat less at night if you weren't eating to distract yourself from any thoughts or feelings?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't think that really applies to me. I'm not... I don't feel like I'm eating to block out any thoughts or feelings. Like I mentioned a minute ago, my mood doesn't really seem to factor into it at all. It's just something that happens when I'm out in the garage late at night. So, no, I don't think that would make a difference either way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your nighttime eating would stop if you found another way to cope with your emotions or deal with boredom?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying, I don't really think it's about my emotions. I'm not upset or trying to cope with anything when I do it. And I wouldn't say I'm bored, either—I'm usually keeping busy tinkering with something out in the garage. So, no, I don't think finding a different way to cope would stop it, just because... well, I'm not really coping with anything to begin with.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I don't really feel stressed or anxious about much these days. I'm not working anymore, so my days are pretty quiet and simple. It doesn't really seem tied to feeling worried or anything like that... I just find myself doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating when you're picking at leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Like I mentioned before, it's like I know I should stop... I tell myself to stop... but I just keep eating anyway. I don't feel like I have any control over it once I get started on those leftovers.\n\nDoctor: Is it always leftovers that you lose control over eating, or can it happen with other types of food as well?\n\nPatient: Well, it usually starts with the leftovers, but then it turns into other things. I'll take some pan dulce out to the garage and eat that, too. I end up eating a whole lot of food—way more than I’d ever actually put on a plate if my family was sitting there watching me.\n\nDoctor: Does this loss of control over eating happen at other times of the day, or is it mostly at night?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just at night. During the day, I eat my normal meals with my wife, maybe a small snack, and I feel fine. I don't feel that urge to just keep going. It's only after supper, when the house is quiet and I'm cleaning up the kitchen or heading out to the garage, that it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating quickly when you're having one of these episodes at night?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I eat quickly. It's more of a... a steady thing. I just keep picking at it, taking another bite, then another. It's not like I'm rushing to get it down, I just can't seem to stop the process once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel very hungry before you start eating those leftovers at night?\n\nPatient: No, a lot of the time I'm not even hungry. I mean, I've already had a good dinner with everyone, so I'm usually pretty full. But later on... I usually just end up having to sit in my workshop chair for a while because my stomach gets so uncomfortable.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat a large amount of food during these episodes, more than you would normally eat in one sitting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say it's a large amount. Some nights after supper, when I’m supposed to be putting away the leftovers—like the tamales or the mole—I’ll end up eating several extra servings. And then I'll usually take some pan dulce out to the garage with me, too. When you add it all up, it's definitely way more food than I’d ever put on a plate if the family was around watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating rapidly during these episodes at night?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I eat fast. I just kind of pick at things... take a bite of this, a piece of that. I'm taking my time with it, really. It's just that I keep eating for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually try to hide or keep secret the fact that you're eating these extra amounts of food at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I make sure I wait until the house is quiet, or I take it out to the garage with me. I'd just be... well, I'd be embarrassed if my cousin or the rest of the family saw how much I was actually eating. I'm supposed to be the disciplined one, you know? So I definitely keep it to myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating at night because you're trying to feel better or comfort yourself in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that. I haven't really thought about it like that, to be honest. I just know I end up eating all that extra food when I'm out there working on my projects or when the house is quiet. I don't know if I'm trying to comfort myself... it's just something I end up doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating at night helps you feel better emotionally?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying, I really don't know. I don't think it makes me feel better, no. I haven't noticed any kind of emotional lift from it or anything like that. It's just... well, it's just a habit I've fallen into when I'm out there. I don't really connect it to my mood.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still eat this way at night if you were in a very good mood?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I probably would. Like I said, I don't really think my mood has much to do with it. Even if I've had a great day, once it gets late and I'm out in the garage... I still end up eating all that extra food. It just seems to happen regardless of how I'm feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat less at night if you weren't alone?\n\nPatient: Oh, definitely. I mean, I actually make sure I do it after the house is quiet or when I'm out in the garage. I'd be really embarrassed for my cousin or the rest of my family to see just how much I'm eating. I'm supposed to be the disciplined one, you know? So, yeah, if someone else was around, I wouldn't be doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're losing control over your eating during the day, not just at night?\n\nPatient: No, not really. During the day, I'm... well, I'm fine. I just eat my regular meals. It's really only at night, like I was saying, when I'm out there by myself. During the day, I don't have that feeling of losing control at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat less at night if you weren't eating to distract yourself from any thoughts or feelings?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't think that really applies to me. I'm not... I don't feel like I'm eating to block out any thoughts or feelings. Like I mentioned a minute ago, my mood doesn't really seem to factor into it at all. It's just something that happens when I'm out in the garage late at night. So, no, I don't think that would make a difference either way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your nighttime eating would stop if you found another way to cope with your emotions or deal with boredom?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying, I don't really think it's about my emotions. I'm not upset or trying to cope with anything when I do it. And I wouldn't say I'm bored, either—I'm usually keeping busy tinkering with something out in the garage. So, no, I don't think finding a different way to cope would stop it, just because... well, I'm not really coping with anything to begin with.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or criticize yourself because you can't stop eating once you start, rather than just feeling guilty about eating late at night?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I don't really beat myself up over not being able to stop. Honestly, I don't even feel like I can't stop... I just eat whatever snacks I took out to the garage with me and that's it. I might feel a little foolish for eating a bunch of junk food that late at night, but I don't sit there criticizing myself for losing control or anything like that."
